==== Summary for ./frontend/src/App.js ====
The `App.js` file serves as the root configuration and routing hub for a multi-role E-Learning platform. It manages global state via providers, defines distinct layouts for public and private views, and implements role-based access control.

### Core Logic & Components

*   **`App` (Root Component):** Wraps the application in essential providers: `ChakraProvider` (UI), `QueryClientProvider` (data fetching), `AuthProvider` (session), and `NotificationProvider` (alerts).
*   **`AppRouter` (Layout Decider):** Dynamically switches between `PublicLayout` and `PrivateLayout`. It checks the current path against a `publicRoutes` array to determine which layout to render based on the URL.
*   **Layouts:**
    *   **`PublicLayout`:** Provides a standard structure (`Navbar`, `Footer`) for landing pages, authentication (Login/Register), and browsing (Courses, Universities, Clans).
    *   **`PrivateLayout`:** Includes a `Sidebar` for navigation within authenticated areas like Dashboards, Profiles, and Management panels.
*   **Role-Based Routing:** Utilizes a `ProtectedRoute` component to enforce access levels:
    *   `requiredStudent`: Access to enrollments and lesson players.
    *   `requiredTeacher`: Access to course creation, management, and reviews.
    *   `requiredAdmin`: Access to user management, university requests, and system-wide dashboards.

### Key Route Categories

*   **Authentication:** Login, Register, Password Recovery.
*   **Academic:** University/Department browsing, Course details, and Lesson playback.
*   **Social:** Clans (creation, members, competitions) and Community (forums, posts).
*   **Management:** Specialized dashboards for Students, Teachers, and Admins.
*   **Utilities:** Payment (Checkout), Profile editing, and 404 handling.

### Key Functions

*   **`setAccessToken(token)`**: Stores a provided token in `localStorage`. It includes a guard clause to prevent storing empty or null values.
*   **`clearAccessToken()`**: Removes the token from `localStorage`, effectively logging the user out or resetting the session.

### Logic Summary
The module abstracts `localStorage` operations into a simple API for authentication state management, ensuring that the access token persists across browser refreshes and sessions.


==== Summary for ./frontend/src/components/AuthRedirect.jsx ====
The `AuthRedirect.jsx` file defines a React component designed to manage access to public-facing routes (like a Landing Page) by redirecting authenticated users to a protected area.

### **Key Component: `AuthRedirect`**
A wrapper component that conditionally renders its children based on the user's authentication state.

### **Logic & Functionality**
*   **Authentication State:** Utilizes the `useAuth` hook to monitor the current `user` object and the `loading` status.
*   **Conditional Redirection:**
    *   Inside a `useEffect` hook, if the authentication process is finished (`!loading`) and a `user` is present, it triggers a redirect to `/home` using `navigate` with the `{ replace: true }` option (to prevent the landing page from staying in the browser history).
*   **Rendering Lifecycle:**
    *   **Loading Phase:** Returns `null` while authentication status is being determined to avoid "flickering" content.
    *   **Authenticated:** Returns `null` (since the user is being moved to `/home`).
    *   **Unauthenticated:** Renders the `children` (e.g., the Landing Page), allowing guests to view public content.

*   **Dynamic Styling:** 
    *   Uses `typeColors` to visually categorize notification types (e.g., green for accepted requests, red for rejected).

==== Summary for ./frontend/src/components/ProtectedRoute.jsx ====
The `ProtectedRoute` component serves as a **route guard** to manage access based on authentication status and user roles.

### Key Logic & Flow:
1.  **Authentication State:** It utilizes the `useAuth` hook to access the current `user` object and `loading` status.
2.  **Loading State:** While the authentication status is being determined, it renders a `<Loading />` component to prevent premature redirects.
3.  **Authentication Check:** If no user is logged in, it redirects the browser to the `/login` page.
4.  **Authorization (Admin):** If the `requiredAdmin` prop is true, it verifies the `user.isAdmin` flag. If the user is not an admin, they are redirected to the home page (`/`).
5.  **Authorization (Role):** If a `requiredRole` is specified, it compares it against `user.role`. Mismatched roles result in a redirect to the home page (`/`).
6.  **Rendering:** If all checks pass, it renders the `children` components passed to it.

### Component API (Props):
*   **`children`**: The component(s) to render if access is granted.
*   **`requiredRole`**: (Optional) A specific string (e.g., `'instructor'`) required to access the route.
*   **`requiredAdmin`**: (Optional) A boolean flag requiring the user to have administrative privileges.

### Core Logic & State
*   **Authentication Integration**: Uses a custom `useAuth` hook to determine if a user is logged in. It conditionally renders "Dashboard/Profile" for authenticated users and "Login/Sign Up" for guests.

==== Summary for ./frontend/src/components/VideoPlayer.jsx ====
This React component, `VideoPlayer`, handles video playback for educational lessons using either local MP4 files or YouTube embeds, while synchronizing progress with a backend service.

### Key Logic & Features
*   **Dual-Player Support:** Dynamically switches between an HTML5 `<video>` tag for MP4s and the YouTube IFrame Player API.
*   **Progress Tracking:** Reports "watched seconds" to the `learningService` every 15 seconds (or on pause/jump) to prevent data loss.
*   **Auto-Completion:** Automatically marks a lesson as completed when the user reaches 90% of the video duration or when the video ends.
*   **Event Communication:** Dispatches browser-level `CustomEvent`s (`lessonProgress`, `lessonCompleted`) so other parts of the UI can react to player updates.

### Key Functions
*   **`sendWatch(watchedSeconds)`**: Calls the backend to record progress, calculates the percentage watched, and updates the local completion state.
*   **`sendComplete()`**: Explicitly notifies the backend and the application that the lesson is finished.
*   **`ensureYouTubeApi()`**: A helper that asynchronously loads the YouTube IFrame API script if it isn't already present on the page.

### Refs & State
*   **`lastSentRef`**: A mutable ref used to throttle progress updates and avoid redundant network calls.
*   **`isCompletedRef`**: Mirrors the `isCompleted` state to ensure callbacks (like the YouTube interval) have access to the most current value without triggering re-renders or suffering from stale closures.

==== Summary for ./frontend/src/context/AuthContext.js ====
This code defines a React Context for managing and providing authentication state across the application.

### **Key Components**
*   **`AuthContext`**: A React Context object created to hold the user's authentication data and loading status.
*   **`AuthProvider`**: A provider component that wraps the application to manage auth state:
    *   **`user`**: Stores the profile data of the authenticated user.
    *   **`loading`**: A boolean flag indicating if the initial profile fetch is in progress.
*   **`useAuth`**: A custom hook that provides a convenient way for components to access the authentication context (`user`, `loading`, and `setUser`).

### **Logic & Execution**
*   **Token Retrieval**: On component mount, it attempts to retrieve an access token using `getAccessToken()`.
*   **Profile Fetching**: If a token is present, it executes an authenticated `GET` request to `/auth/profile` via `apiFetch`.
*   **State Updates**:
    *   If the request succeeds, `user` is updated with the profile data.
    *   If the request fails or no token exists, `user` remains `null`.
    *   `loading` is set to `false` once the check is complete, regardless of the outcome.

==== Summary for ./frontend/src/context/NotificationContext.js ====
This file implements a global notification system using the **React Context API**. It allows components to trigger and manage toast-style messages from anywhere in the application.

### **Key Components**
*   **`NotificationContext`**: The context object used by consumer components to access notification state and methods.
*   **`NotificationProvider`**: A wrapper component that manages the notification state and provides it to its children.

### **Key Functions**
*   **`addNotification(message, type, duration)`**: 
    *   Creates a notification object with a unique ID (`Date.now()`).
    *   Appends it to the `notifications` state array.
    *   If `duration > 0`, it triggers an automatic cleanup via `setTimeout`.
    *   Returns the notification ID for manual reference.
*   **`removeNotification(id)`**: 
    *   Filters the state to remove the notification matching the provided ID.
    *   Memoized with `useCallback` to prevent unnecessary re-renders in child components.

### **Core Logic**
*   **State Management**: Notifications are stored as an array of objects containing `id`, `message`, and `type` (defaulting to `'info'`).
*   **Automatic Cleanup**: The system handles its own lifecycle by automatically removing notifications after a default 3-second (3000ms) interval, ensuring the UI doesn't become cluttered.
*   **Performance**: Uses `useCallback` for the management functions to ensure they remain stable across re-renders when passed into the Context Provider.

==== Summary for ./frontend/src/hooks/useFetch.js ====
This code defines a custom React hook, `useFetch`, designed to handle data fetching logic within functional components.

### Key Logic & Structure:

*   **`useFetch` Hook:**
    *   **State Management:** It maintains three core states: `data` (initialized as `null`), `loading` (initialized as `true`), and `error` (initialized as `null`).
    *   **Side Effect (`useEffect`):** Executes an asynchronous `fetchData` function whenever the provided `fetchFn` dependency changes.
    *   **Asynchronous Flow:**
        1.  Sets `loading` to `true`.
        2.  Executes the provided `fetchFn`.
        3.  On success: Updates `data` with the result and clears any previous `error`.
        4.  On failure: Catches the `err`, updates the `error` state, and resets `data` to `null`.
        5.  Finalization: Sets `loading` to `false` regardless of the outcome.
*   **Return Value:** Returns an object containing the current `data`, `loading` status, and any `error` encountered, allowing components to reactively render UI based on the fetch state.


==== Summary for ./frontend/src/hooks/useForm.js ====


==== Summary for ./frontend/src/hooks/useNotification.js ====
The provided code defines a custom React hook, `useNotification`, which simplifies access to notification-related state and functions within the application.

### Key Logic & Components:

*   **`useNotification` (Custom Hook):**
    *   **Context Access:** Uses React's `useContext` to consume the `NotificationContext`.
    *   **Safety Check:** Validates that the hook is being used within a `NotificationProvider`. If accessed outside this provider, it throws a descriptive error to prevent runtime failures.
    *   **Return Value:** Returns the context object, which typically contains methods (like `showNotification`) and the current state of notifications.

### Summary
This hook acts as a consumer for the `NotificationContext`, providing a clean and safe interface for components to interact with the notification system without needing to manually import the context or handle null checks repeatedly.

==== Summary for ./frontend/src/pages/Admin/CompetitionManagement.jsx ====
The `CompetitionManagement` component is a React-based administrative dashboard used to oversee, approve, and manage competitions. It is built using **Chakra UI** for styling and **React Icons** for visual indicators.

### **Key Functions**

#### **Data Fetching & State Management**
*   **`fetchCompetitions`**: An asynchronous function that retrieves all competitions from the `/competitions/admin/all-competitions` endpoint. It also calculates and sets the global statistics (Total, Pending, Approved, Rejected).
*   **`filterCompetitions`**: Logic that runs whenever the search text or status filter changes. It performs client-side filtering on the `competitions` array to update the displayed list.

#### **Action Handlers**
*   **`handleApprove(id)`**: Sends a POST request to approve a pending competition and refreshes the data.
*   **`handleReject(id)`**: Sends a POST request with a user-provided `rejectReason` to mark a competition as rejected.
*   **`handleDelete(id)`**: Triggers a deletion request after a browser confirmation, removing the competition from the system.

#### **UI Helpers**
*   **`getStatusBadge`**: Returns a color-coded Chakra `Badge` (Green for Approved, Red for Rejected, Yellow for Pending).
*   **`getCompetitionTypeBadge`**: Maps competition categories (e.g., Academic, Quiz, Technical) to specific color schemes.

### **Core Logic & Flow**

1.  **Initial Load**: On mount (`useEffect`), the component fetches the full list of competitions and generates counts for the stats cards.
2.  **Filtering & Search**: Users can filter by status (All, Pending, Approved, Rejected) or search by Title and Creator name. This is handled by a `useEffect` hook that listens to changes in the search/filter state.
3.  **Modal Interactions**: 
    *   **View Modal**: Displays comprehensive details about a selected competition, including creator role and date ranges.
    *   **Reject Modal**: Captures a mandatory or optional reason string before calling the rejection API.
4.  **Feedback**: Uses the Chakra `useToast` hook to provide real-time success or error notifications for every administrative action.

### **Key UI Components**
*   **Stats Grid**: A `SimpleGrid` showing four summary cards with color-coded numbers.
*   **Filters Card**: Contains a search `Input` and a status `Select` dropdown.
*   **Management Table**: A responsive `Table` displaying competition metadata and action buttons (View, Approve, Reject, Delete).
*   **Action Loading**: Uses an `actionLoading` state to disable buttons and show spinners during API calls to prevent duplicate submissions.

==== Summary for ./frontend/src/pages/Admin/UniversityManagement.jsx ====
This React component, `UniversityManagement`, provides a user interface for creating a university along with its associated departments. It uses **Chakra UI** for styling and **Axios** (via a custom `api` service) for backend communication.

### Key Logic and Functions

*   **State Management:**
    *   Manages basic university fields (`name`, `code`, `bannerFile`).
    *   Uses an array state (`departments`) to dynamically track a list of department objects, each containing its own name, code, description, and banner image.

*   **Department CRUD Operations:**
    *   `addDepartment`: Appends a new department template to the list.
    *   `removeDepartment`: Removes a department entry by index.
    *   `updateDepartment` / `updateDepartmentFile`: Handlers for updating specific fields or files within a specific department object in the state array.

*   **Form Submission (`handleCreate`):**
    1.  **Validation:** Ensures university name and code are provided.
    2.  **University Banner Upload:** If a file is selected, it uploads the image to `/uploads/image` using `FormData` and retrieves the URL.
    3.  **University Creation:** Sends a `POST` request to `/universities` with the name, code, and banner URL.
    4.  **Department Processing:** Upon successful university creation, it iterates through the `departments` array:
        *   Uploads the department-specific banner image (if present).
        *   Sends a `POST` request to `/departmentrequests` for each department, linking them to the newly created university via its ID.
    5.  **Feedback & Cleanup:** Uses `useToast` to notify the user of success or failure and resets the form state upon a successful transaction.

### Component Structure
*   **University Section:** Input fields for the university's primary identification and a file picker for the banner.
*   **Dynamic Department List:** A map of the `departments` state, rendering sub-forms for each department added.
*   **Submission Control:** A primary action button with a loading state to prevent duplicate submissions during asynchronous API calls.

    2.  Calls `authService.register` with the formatted payload.
    3.  On success: Displays a success toast and redirects the user to the `/login` page.

*   **`passwordStrength`**: A derived variable that categorizes the password as "Weak", "Good", or "Strong" based on length.

    *   **Social Auth**: UI buttons for Google and GitHub.

==== Summary for ./frontend/src/pages/Clans/ClanCommunity.jsx ====
The `ClanCommunity.jsx` file implements a social feed for a clan-based system, allowing members to share posts, react with emojis, and vote on content.

### **Core Components**

*   **`ClanCommunity` (Main Component):**
    *   Acts as the parent container and state manager.
    *   Uses **React Query** (`useQuery`) to fetch posts for a specific `clanId`.
    *   Manages mutations for voting and adding emoji reactions.
    *   Renders a header with a "New Post" trigger and maps through the list of posts.

*   **`PostCard`:**
    *   Displays individual post data including the author's avatar, name, role, and timestamp.
    *   Handles the display of post metadata like `viewCount` and `isPinned` status.
    *   Renders post content (text and optional images).
    *   Provides interactive buttons for **Upvoting/Downvoting**, **Commenting**, and **Emoji Reactions**.

*   **`CreatePostModal`:**
    *   A form-driven modal for submitting new content.
    *   Utilizes a `Textarea` for the post body.
    *   Triggers a POST request to the backend and invalidates the `clanPosts` query on success to refresh the feed.

### **Key Logic & State Management**

*   **Data Fetching & Synchronization:** Uses `@tanstack/react-query` to handle asynchronous API calls. It automatically refreshes the UI after actions (posting, voting, reacting) by invalidating the cache.
*   **Voting Logic:** Supports three states (Upvote, Downvote, or Neutral). The `onVote` function sends the user's choice to the backend to update counts dynamically.
*   **Reaction System:** Users can select from a predefined list of emojis (`EMOJI_OPTIONS`). The UI displays existing reaction counts with tooltips showing which users reacted.
*   **Navigation:** Uses `useNavigate` to redirect users to author profiles when clicking on avatars or names.
*   **UI/UX:** Built with **Chakra UI**, implementing responsive layouts, dark/light mode support (via `useColorModeValue`), and feedback via `useToast` for API errors or successes.

### **Key API Interactions**
*   `GET /clans/${clanId}/posts`: Retrieves the feed.
*   `POST /clans/${clanId}/posts`: Creates a new post.
*   `POST /clans/${clanId}/posts/${postId}/react`: Toggles an emoji reaction.
*   `POST /clans/${clanId}/posts/${postId}/vote`: Updates the upvote/downvote status.

This file implements the **ClanDetail** component, a comprehensive dashboard for viewing and managing a specific clan in an E-Learning platform. It uses **Chakra UI** for styling, **React Query** for state management, and **React Router** for navigation.

    - Fetches clan details, members, statistics, competitions, and pending join requests.
    - Uses `queryKey` invalidation to refresh data after mutations (like joining or leaving).

    *   A sub-component that renders individual member profiles.
    *   Displays user information (Avatar, Name), role-specific badges, and activity statistics (contribution points, posts, weekly/monthly points).
    *   Contains a conditional **moderation menu** (via `Menu` and `MenuItem`) visible only to users with management permissions.

### Key Functions & Logic

*   **Data Fetching:**
    *   `fetchClanMembers`: An asynchronous function that retrieves member data from the API endpoint `/clans/{clanId}/members`.
*   **State Management (React Query):**
    *   `useQuery`: Fetches and caches the member list using the `clanMembers` key.
    *   `updateRoleMutation`: Handles promoting members to "Elder" or "Co-Leader" via a `PUT` request.
    *   `removeMemberMutation`: Handles member expulsion via a `DELETE` request.
*   **Filtering & Searching:**
    *   Implements local filtering of the `members` array based on `searchQuery` (case-insensitive name match) and `roleFilter` (exact match).
*   **Permissions Logic:**
    *   `canManageMembers`: A boolean derived from the member list that checks if the logged-in user holds the "Leader" or "Co-Leader" role.
    *   Guard clauses prevent the "Leader" from being removed or having their role modified through the standard UI.
*   **Stats Aggregation:**
    *   Calculates `roleCounts` using a `reduce` function to show how many members occupy each specific role (Leader, Elder, etc.).

### UI/UX Features

*   **Responsive Design:** Uses `SimpleGrid` to adapt the layout for mobile (1 column) and desktop (3 columns).
*   **Loading States:** Employs Chakra's `Skeleton` and `SkeletonText` to provide visual feedback during data fetching.
*   **Feedback:** Uses `useToast` to provide real-time success or error notifications for administrative actions.
*   **Theming:** Utilizes `useColorModeValue` to ensure compatibility with both light and dark modes.

==== Summary for ./frontend/src/pages/Community/PostCard.jsx ====
The `PostCard` component is a React-based UI element designed to display and interact with community posts. It utilizes **Chakra UI** for styling, **React Query** for data synchronization, and **React Router** for navigation.

### **Key Components**
*   **`PostCard` (Main Component):** Manages the display of individual posts, including user metadata, content, and interactive actions (likes, dislikes, comments).
*   **`PostMedia` (Helper Component):** Safely renders post images using `normalizeUrl`. It includes error handling to hide the component if the image fails to load.

### **Key Logic & State Management**
*   **ID Normalization:** Dynamically extracts the post ID (`pid`) from various possible naming conventions (e.g., `Id`, `id`, `_id`, `postId`) to ensure compatibility with different API response shapes.
*   **Optimistic Updates:** The `likeMutation` utilizes optimistic UI updates. It immediately toggles the "liked" state and increments/decrements the count locally before the server responds, reverting the change if the request fails.
*   **Query Invalidation:** After successful mutations (like, dislike, delete, or edit), the component invalidates `communityPosts` and `myPosts` caches in React Query to ensure the UI reflects the latest global state.
*   **Ownership Check:** Compares the `userId` from `localStorage` with the post's owner ID to conditionally render "Edit" and "Delete" administrative menus.

### **Key Functions & Mutations**
*   **`likeMutation` / `handleLike`:** Toggles the upvote status. Requires a valid auth token; otherwise, it triggers a warning toast.
*   **`dislikeMutation` / `handleDislike`:** Handles downvoting logic and increments the downvote counter.
*   **`deleteMutation` / `handleDelete`:** Triggers a window confirmation before calling the API to permanently remove a post.
*   **`handleEditSuccess`:** A callback that closes the edit modal and refreshes the post list upon a successful update.
*   **`handleViewDetail`:** Navigates the user to the dedicated detailed view of the post.

### **UI Features**
*   **Conditional Rendering:** Shows a `Menu` for post owners to edit or delete their content.
*   **Interactive Header:** Clicking the Avatar or Username navigates to the specific user's profile.
*   **Modal Integration:** Integrates the `CreatePost` component in "Edit Mode" to allow inline updates to title, content, and category.
*   **Badges & Icons:** Displays post categories/types using Chakra `Badge` and timestamps with `TimeIcon`.

==== Summary for ./frontend/src/pages/Competitions/ClanVsClansCompetitionCreate.jsx ====
This React component, `ClanVsClansCompetitionCreate`, provides a multi-step form for clan leaders to create and challenge another clan to a competitive programming-themed quiz.

### **Key Features & Logic**

*   **Role-Based Access Control:** 
    *   Requires the user to be authenticated.
    *   Restricts creation to users who are leaders or co-leaders of at least one clan (verified via the `userClans` state).
*   **Multi-Step Wizard:** Uses a `currentStep` state and Chakra UI `Tabs` to navigate through three stages: `details`, `questions`, and `review`.
*   **Dynamic Question Management:** 
    *   Allows users to add or remove multiple MCQ questions.
    *   Each question tracks topic, points, four options, and the correct answer.
*   **Context-Aware Data Fetching:** On mount, it calls `/api/competitions/create-context` to retrieve clans the user manages (challengers) and available clans to challenge (opponents).
*   **Validation Logic:** 
    *   `validateDetails()`: Ensures title, opponent selection, participant counts (1-10), and duration (5-180 mins) are valid.
    *   `validateQuestions()`: Ensures every question has text, four options, and a valid correct answer.

### **Primary Functions**

*   **`handleFormChange` / `handleQuestionChange`**: Update the local state for competition metadata and the array of quiz questions respectively.
*   **`handleSubmit`**: 
    *   Aggregates `formData` and `questions`.
    *   Performs an asynchronous POST request to `/api/clan-vs-clans-competitions`.
    *   On success, redirects the user to the newly created competition's detail page.

    - `selectedParticipants`: Manages a local list of member IDs for participant selection logic.
    - `actionLoading`: Prevents duplicate submissions during API calls.
- **Conditional Rendering:**
    - **Pending State:** Shows "Accept" and "Reject" buttons to the opponent leader.
    - **Scheduled/Ready State:** Displays a selection interface allowing leaders to pick exactly `participantsPerClan` members.
    - **Completed State:** Displays a results card showing scores and the winning clan.
- **Modal Integration:** Uses `useDisclosure` to manage a modal for entering a rejection reason when declining a challenge.

- **UI:** Chakra UI for layout, cards, and feedback (toasts/spinners).

- **Auth/API:** Custom `useAuth` hook for user context and `competitionApi` for backend communication.

*   **`handleClanSelect/Remove`**: Adds or removes entire clans.

*   **`handleQuestionChange`**: Updates a specific field (e.g., Option A, Correct Answer) for a specific question in the array based on its index.
*   **Question Structure**: Each question is rendered as a distinct card with inputs for text, options, and a dropdown for the correct answer.

#### **4. Form Submission (`handleSubmit`)**
*   Aggregates all state data (form fields, questions, selected members, and selected clans).
*   Sends a POST request to the backend.
*   Includes navigation logic to redirect the user back to the competition list upon success.

    *   **Metadata**: Title, description, and duration (formatted as `MM:SS`).

    *   **Selection State**: Highlights the currently active lecture with a `green.50` background and toggles the button variant from `ghost` to `solid`.
*   **Navigation**: The `setSelectedPartIndex` callback is triggered when a user clicks "Play," allowing a parent component to update the video player.

    *   If the course has a **price**, it redirects the user to the `/payment` page.
    *   If the course is **free**, it calls the enrollment API directly. Upon success, it refreshes the course data and shows a success toast.
*   **Rating & Review (`submitRating`):** Manages the submission of a numerical star rating and an optional text review. It validates that a rating is selected before calling the API and refreshes the UI to reflect the new average rating.
*   **Conditional Content Rendering:**
    *   **Enrolled View:** If the user is enrolled, it displays a list of lessons (valid video parts) that link to individual lesson pages.
    *   **Public/Locked View:** If not enrolled, it shows a "Course Preview" placeholder with a lock icon and an enrollment prompt.
    *   **Video Handling:** Includes logic to detect and embed YouTube URLs or standard video files for previews.

### **State Management**
*   **`course`**: Stores the complete course object (title, description, price, instructor, etc.).
*   **`loading` / `submitting`**: Boolean flags to manage UI feedback during API calls.
*   **`rating` / `review`**: Local state for the feedback form inputs.

### **UI Components (Chakra UI)**
*   **Layout:** Uses a `Grid` layout with a main content area (left) and a sticky sidebar (right).
*   **Feedback:** Employs `Skeleton` for loading states, `Toast` for notifications, and `Badges` for course status/levels.
*   **Data Display:** Uses `Card`, `Stack`, and `Icon` components to organize metadata like university name, instructor, student count, and duration.
*   **Interactive:** Features a star-based rating selector and responsive buttons for enrollment.

### **External Dependencies**
*   **`react-router-dom`**: For URL parameters (`useParams`) and navigation (`useNavigate`).
*   **`courseService` / `api`**: Custom service modules for handling backend communication.
*   **`react-icons` / `@chakra-ui/icons`**: For visual context (clock, users, books, stars).

### Component Summary: `CourseList`

`CourseList` is a React functional component designed to browse and filter courses within an E-Learning platform.

#### Key Functions
- **`fetchCourses(universityId)`**: 
    - Fetches courses from `courseService` with optional university filtering.

- **`fetchUniversities()`**: 
    - Fetches the list of universities from the API to populate the filter dropdown.
    - Maps diverse backend property casings (e.g., `id` vs. `Id`) to a consistent local format.

#### State & Logic
- **Lifecycle Management**: Uses `useEffect` to trigger initial data fetching for both universities and courses upon component mounting.
- **Filtering**: Tracks `selectedUniversity` and re-triggers `fetchCourses` whenever the dropdown value changes.
- **Data Robustness**: Throughout the component, logical ORs (e.g., `course.id || course.courseId`) are used to gracefully handle variations in backend naming conventions.

#### UI Structure
- **State Feedback**: Displays a `<Loading />` component or a red error message depending on the fetch status.
- **Filter Bar**: Contains a `<select>` dropdown to filter the course grid by university.
- **Courses Grid**: 
    - Renders a responsive grid of `Card` components.
    - Each card displays a thumbnail, title, description, university name, and difficulty level.
- **Navigation**: Uses `useNavigate` to redirect users to specific course detail pages when the "View Course" button is clicked.

==== Summary for ./frontend/src/pages/Dashboard/Dashboard.jsx ====
The `Dashboard.jsx` file defines a central **`Dashboard`** component that acts as a router-level view to conditionally render specific dashboards based on user roles and state.

### Key Logic & Components

*   **Role-Based Rendering:**
    *   **Admin:** Renders `AdminDashboard` if `user.isAdmin` is true.
    *   **Teacher:** Renders `TeacherDashboard` if the user has teacher privileges (`user.isTeacher`) AND the `activeRole` in local storage is explicitly set to `'teacher'`.
    *   **Student:** Renders `StudentDashboard` as the default fallback. This covers both standard students and multi-role users (like teachers) who are currently in student mode.
*   **Data Sources:**
    *   **`useAuth` Hook:** Provides the current `user` object containing role flags (`isAdmin`, `isTeacher`).
    *   **Local Storage:** Checks `'activeRole'` to determine which dashboard to show for users who possess multiple roles (e.g., Teacher vs. Student mode).
*   **UI Strategy:**
    *   The component uses **conditional rendering** rather than navigation redirects. This approach is explicitly intended to preserve browser history and ensure the "Back" button functions correctly for the user.

This file defines the **`StudentDashboard`** component, which serves as the primary hub for students to track their learning progress, course enrollments, and competition activities.

### **1. Key Components**

#### **`StudentDashboard` (Main Functional Component)**

==== Summary for ./frontend/src/pages/Learning/LessonPlayer.jsx ====
This code file defines the **`LessonPlayer`** component, which serves as the primary interface for viewing and interacting with individual educational lessons.

### **Key Components & Services**
*   **`LessonPlayer`**: The main functional component that manages the lesson state, navigation, and layout.
*   **`learningService`**: An external service used to fetch lesson data (`getLesson`) from the backend.
*   **Integrated Components**:
    *   **`VideoPlayer`**: Handles the actual video playback logic.
    *   **`ProgressTracker`**: Displays and manages the user's progress within the specific lesson.

### **Core Logic & State Management**
*   **Data Fetching**: Uses `useEffect` to fetch lesson details whenever the `lessonId` (from the URL) changes. It handles loading states and error reporting via Chakra UI toasts.
*   **Automatic Navigation**: Listens for a custom window event (`lessonCompleted`). When triggered, it validates the `NextLessonId` and automatically navigates the user to the next lesson after a brief delay.
*   **Navigation Validation**: A specialized `useEffect` proactively checks if the previous and next lessons actually exist. This prevents "404 Not Found" errors by validating IDs before the user attempts to click navigation buttons.
*   **Video Type Inference**: Contains logic to normalize the video provider. If the backend doesn't explicitly provide a `VideoType`, the component inspects the `VideoUrl` to determine if it is a **YouTube** video or a direct **MP4** file.

### **Key Functions**
*   **`formatDuration`**: A helper function that converts raw seconds into a human-readable "X mins" string.
*   **`validate`**: An internal asynchronous function within `useEffect` that verifies the availability of neighboring lessons to ensure smooth navigation.

### **UI Structure**
*   **Breadcrumb Navigation**: Shows the path from "Learning" to the specific lesson.
*   **Lesson Header**: Displays the title, duration, completion status, and video source type.
*   **Main Content Area**: Houses the `VideoPlayer` and the `ProgressTracker`.
*   **Sidebar/Mobile Nav**: Provides quick actions (like "Restart Lesson") and navigation controls to move between lessons.

==== Summary for ./frontend/src/pages/Learning/ProgressTracker.jsx ====
This `ProgressTracker` component manages and displays video lesson progress by listening for custom window events.

### Key Logic & Functions
- **State Management:** Uses a `state` object to store progress data (watched seconds, percentage, completion status) indexed by `lessonId`.
- **Event Listeners (`useEffect`):**
    - `lessonProgress`: Updates the state for a specific lesson with current watch time and percentage.
    - `lessonCompleted`: Marks a specific lesson as finished (100% progress).
    - **Cleanup:** Removes listeners when the component unmounts to prevent memory leaks.
- **Conditional Rendering:**
    - **Individual View:** If a `lessonId` is provided, it displays a detailed progress card for that specific lesson.
    - **Global View:** If no `lessonId` is provided, it renders a list (`VStack`) of all lessons currently tracked in the state.

### UI Components (Chakra UI)
- **`Progress`:** Visualizes the completion percentage using a purple progress bar.
- **`Box` / `VStack`:** Handles layout, padding, and background styling for the tracker cards.
- **`Text`:** Displays numeric progress (minutes watched and percentage) and "Completed" status.

==== Summary for ./frontend/src/pages/Learning/YouTubeLessonPlayer.jsx ====
The `YouTubeLessonPlayer` component is a React-based video player designed for an E-Learning platform. It integrates the YouTube IFrame API to embed videos and provides real-time progress tracking and automatic lesson completion.

### **Key Functions & Logic**

#### **1. URL Parsing (`extractYouTubeId`)**
*   A utility function that uses both `URL` API and Regular Expressions to extract the unique YouTube Video ID from various URL formats (e.g., `youtu.be`, `youtube.com/watch?v=...`).

#### **2. Initialization & API Loading**
*   **Dynamic Script Injection:** If the YouTube IFrame API isn't already present, it dynamically injects the script into the document.
*   **Player Setup:** Once the API is ready, it initializes a `window.YT.Player` instance attached to a local `div` ref.

#### **3. Progress Tracking Logic**
*   **Polling Interval:** Sets up a 1-second interval to query the player's current time (`getCurrentTime`).
*   **Throttled API Calls:** Every 5 seconds of watch time, it calls `learningService.watchLesson` to persist progress to the backend.
*   **Event Dispatching:** Fires a `lessonProgress` Custom Event so other parts of the UI can react to the user's progress.

#### **4. Completion Logic**
*   **Auto-Mark (90%):** If `autoMark` is enabled, the component automatically triggers `learningService.completeLesson` once the user has watched 90% of the video duration.
*   **Video End:** When the YouTube player state changes to `ENDED`, it immediately marks the lesson as 100% complete and triggers the completion API.
*   **User Feedback:** Displays a Chakra UI toast notification when a lesson is successfully completed.

#### **5. Cleanup**
*   The `useEffect` cleanup return ensures that the player instance is destroyed, intervals are cleared, and the temporary API script is removed to prevent memory leaks and orphaned listeners.

### **UI Components (Chakra UI)**
*   **Video Container:** A ref-managed `Box` where the IFrame is injected.
*   **Progress Bar:** A `Progress` component that visually represents the percentage of the video watched.
*   **Stats:** Displays the minutes watched and percentage text.

==== Summary for ./frontend/src/pages/Profile/ChangePassword.jsx ====
This React component, `ChangePassword`, provides a secure interface for users to update their account passwords using **Chakra UI** for styling and **Axios** for API communication.

### Key Functions and Logic

*   **`getPasswordStrength()`**: 
    *   Calculates a password's strength based on length, uppercase letters, and numbers.
    *   Returns a score (0-100), a descriptive label (Weak, Fair, Good, Strong), and a corresponding color for the UI progress bar.
*   **`validateForm()`**: 
    *   Performs client-side validation to ensure all fields are filled.
    *   Enforces a minimum length of 6 characters for the new password.
    *   Verifies that the new password is different from the current one and matches the confirmation field.
*   **`handleSubmit()`**: 
    *   An asynchronous function that triggers after validation.
    *   Retrieves a JWT token from `localStorage` and sends a POST request to the `/api/auth/change-password` endpoint.
    *   On success, it displays a toast notification, resets the form, and redirects the user to the `/profile` page.
*   **`handleChange()`**: 
    *   Updates the local state as the user types and dynamically clears error messages for the field being edited.

### UI and Components

*   **Form Controls**: Utilizes Chakra's `FormControl`, `InputGroup`, and `InputRightElement` to create polished input fields with "show/hide password" toggles (using `FaEye` and `FaEyeSlash` icons).
*   **Visual Feedback**: 
    *   Displays real-time **Password Strength** via a `Progress` bar.
    *   Uses **Loading States** on the submit button to prevent multiple submissions.
    *   Employs **Toast Notifications** for immediate success or error feedback.
*   **Layout**: Wraps the form in a centered `Card` with responsive padding and color mode support (`useColorModeValue`) to handle light and dark themes.
*   **Navigation**: Includes a "Back" button that utilizes `useNavigate` to return the user to the profile edit screen.


==== Summary for ./frontend/src/pages/Profile/EditProfile.jsx ====
The `EditProfile` component is a React-based form that allows users to update their personal information, including names, contact details, and profile/cover images.

### Key Functions and Logic

#### 1. Data Retrieval (`fetchProfile`)
*   **Purpose:** Fetches the current user's profile data from the backend (`/api/auth/profile`) upon component mount.
*   **Logic:** Uses an `Authorization: Bearer` token. If the API call fails, it falls back to data stored in `localStorage` to ensure the form is populated.

#### 2. Image Management (`handleImageChange` & `handleCoverChange`)
*   **Validation:** Checks for valid image MIME types and enforces size limits (5MB for profile pictures, 8MB for cover photos).
*   **Preview:** Uses `URL.createObjectURL(file)` to show immediate visual feedback of selected images before they are uploaded to the server.

#### 3. Form Validation (`validateForm`)
*   Ensures required fields (`firstName`, `lastName`, `email`, `username`) are not empty.
*   Uses regex to validate email format and ensures the username is at least 3 characters long.

#### 4. Submission Logic (`handleSubmit`)
*   **Sequential Uploads:** If new images are selected, it first calls a dedicated upload endpoint (`/api/auth/upload-image`) using `FormData` to get permanent URLs.
*   **Profile Update:** Sends a `PUT` request to the profile endpoint with the combined text data and image URLs.
*   **Persistence & Sync:** 
    *   Updates the user data in `localStorage`.
    *   Dispatches a custom `profileUpdated` window event to notify other application components (like navbars or post feeds) to refresh the user's avatar.
    *   Redirects the user back to the profile view upon success.

### State Management
*   **`formData`**: An object containing the textual profile fields.
*   **`profileFile` / `coverFile`**: Stores the actual `File` objects for multi-part uploads.
*   **`preview` / `coverPreview`**: Stores local object URLs for UI previews.
*   **`isLoading` / `isSaving`**: Boolean flags to manage loading spinners and disable buttons during asynchronous operations.

### UI Components (Chakra UI)
*   **`Avatar` & `Image`**: Displays the profile and cover photos with overlayed `IconButton` triggers for file inputs.
*   **`FormControl`**: Handles error state styling and validation messages for inputs.
*   **`Card`**: Containers that separate the "Edit Profile" form from the "Security" (password change) section.

==== Summary for ./frontend/src/pages/Profile/MyEnrollments.jsx ====
The `MyEnrollments.jsx` file defines a React functional component that displays a list of courses a user is currently enrolled in. It features data normalization logic, responsive UI using Chakra UI, and real-time update capabilities.

### Key Functions and Logic

*   **`fetchEnrollments` (Async Function):**
    *   Retrieves enrollment data from the `/enrollments` endpoint.
    *   **Data Normalization:** Includes robust logic to map various backend naming conventions (e.g., `Id` vs `id`, `CourseTitle` vs `title`) into a consistent local object structure.
    *   **Progress Calculation:** Dynamically computes `completedLessons` if the value is missing by using the `ProgressPercentage` and `TotalLessons`.
*   **Event-Driven Updates (`useEffect`):**
    *   Listens for a custom `enrollmentUpdated` window event. When triggered, it refreshes the enrollment list after a small delay (800ms) to ensure backend database consistency.
*   **State Management:**
    *   `enrollments`: An array storing the normalized list of course objects.
    *   `isLoading`: A boolean to toggle a full-screen spinner during data fetching.

### UI and Components

*   **Responsive Grid:** Uses Chakra UI's `Grid` to display course cards in a 1, 2, or 3-column layout depending on the screen size.
*   **Course Cards:** Each card displays:
    *   **Visuals:** A progress ribbon (top-right), course banner image (or a gradient placeholder), and an instructor avatar.
    *   **Status Indicators:** Badges for course status (e.g., "In Progress") and a rating display.
    *   **Progress Tracking:** A visual `Progress` bar and a text-based ratio of completed vs. total lessons.
*   **Navigation:** Provides a "Back to Profile" button and a "Continue Learning" button that routes users to specific course details.
*   **Empty State:** Displays a call-to-action (CTA) with a "Browse More Courses" button if the user has no enrollments.

### Dependencies
*   **Styling:** `@chakra-ui/react` for layout and theming (handling light/dark modes).
*   **Icons:** `react-icons/fa` (Font Awesome).
*   **Context/Services:** `useAuth` for user data and a custom `api` service for HTTP requests.


==== Summary for ./frontend/src/pages/Profile/PublicUserProfile.jsx ====


==== Summary for ./frontend/src/pages/Profile/UserProfile.jsx ====
This `UserProfile` component manages and displays a comprehensive user profile page within a React application using **Chakra UI**.

### Key Functions & Logic

*   **`fetchProfileData` (Async):**
    *   Fetches the main profile data from `/auth/profile`.
    *   Attempts to fetch dashboard statistics and clan memberships from `/auth/dashboard` or `/clans/my-clans`.
    *   Implements a fallback mechanism: if API calls fail, it attempts to load data from `localStorage` to ensure the UI remains functional.
*   **`normalizeProfile(p)`:**
    *   Acts as a robust data mapper. It reconciles various backend naming conventions (e.g., `FirstName` vs `firstName`, `ProfileImageUrl` vs `avatar`) into a consistent object structure for the UI.
*   **`determineRole(clan)`:**
    *   Contains logic to identify the user's specific role within a clan (Leader, Co-Leader, or Member) by comparing IDs or checking specific role fields across different possible data shapes.

### State Management

*   **`profile`**: Stores the normalized user information (name, email, roles, images).
*   **`stats`**: Tracks academic progress metrics like `enrolledCourses` and `completedCourses`.
*   **`myClans`**: An array containing the list of clans the user is currently a member of.
*   **`isLoading`**: Manages the global loading spinner state.

### UI Structure

1.  **Hero Header:** A visually prominent section displaying a cover image, profile avatar, full name, and username. It includes badges indicating user roles (Student, Teacher, Admin, Competitor).
2.  **Action Bar:** Provides buttons for "Edit Profile" and a "Teacher Application" modal (conditional based on the user's current status).
3.  **Statistics Grid:** Displays summary cards for the user's course activity (Enrolled and Completed).
4.  **My Clans Section:** A dynamic area that lists all the user's clans. Each clan card displays the clan's banner, logo, name, tag, user's role, and membership statistics, linking directly to the specific clan page.

### Key Dependencies
*   **Chakra UI**: Used for layout, styling, and responsive design.
*   **React Router (`useNavigate`)**: Handles navigation to profile editing and clan details.
*   **Custom Hooks (`useAuth`)**: Accesses the current authenticated user's context.
*   **React Icons**: Provides visual indicators for profile fields and stats.

==== Summary for ./frontend/src/pages/Teacher/CreateCourse.jsx ====
This code file defines a React component (likely `CourseCreate` or `CourseForm`) for instructors to create or edit a course. It uses **Chakra UI** for the interface and **Axios** for API interactions.

### Key Logic & Features

1.  **University & Department Selection**:
    *   Implements a searchable university selection system where users can filter and select a university from a list.
    *   **Conditional Fetching**: Departments are dynamically loaded and displayed only after a university is selected.
2.  **Course Metadata Management**:
    *   Handles form state for basic course info: Category, Difficulty Level, Language, Course Type, and Description.
    *   **Pricing Logic**: Includes a toggle for "Free Course"; if unchecked, it reveals a numeric input for the course price.
3.  **Media Upload Handling**:
    *   **Image Upload**: Supports both direct URL input and local file uploads via a hidden file input, sending data to `/api/uploads/image`.
    *   **Video Uploads**: Supports local video uploads and YouTube links. 
    *   **Client-side Validation**: Includes logic to check video duration (max 5 minutes) using `onloadedmetadata` before uploading to `/api/uploads/video`.
4.  **Course Content Structure (Course Parts)**:
    *   Manages an array of "Course Parts" or modules.
    *   Each part contains a title, description, and its own video (uploaded or linked).
    *   Includes a dynamic `updatePart` function to modify specific items in the parts list by index.

### Primary Components Used
*   **Layout/Form**: `FormControl`, `FormLabel`, `Input`, `Select`, `SimpleGrid`, `Box`, `Flex`.
*   **UI Elements**: `Badge`, `Spinner`, `Card`, `Checkbox`, `Divider`, `Button`.
*   **Icons**: `FiUpload` for media management.
*   **Feedback**: Uses `useToast` to provide success/error messages for uploads and validation.

### State Dependencies
*   `formData`: Tracks the main course attributes.
*   `videoParts`: An array of objects representing the curriculum modules.
*   `selectedUniversity` & `departments`: Manage the academic hierarchy selection.
*   `universityQuery`: Controls the search filtering for universities.
